People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I came in because... well, because I keep hearing voices, and it’s gotten so clear that I can’t just brush it off anymore. It sounds like somebody is standing right there in my little studio with me, like not a thought in my head, not like remembering somebody’s voice — I mean clear, out loud, like a person in the room.

They’ll say my name, and then they’ll say things about my work being judged. And I’ll stop what I’m doing and look around, because it feels that real, but the house is quiet. Nobody’s there. I check, and there’s no one. It’s really upsetting because I’ll be trying to go about my day, and then there it is again, like I’m being watched or evaluated somehow. That’s what finally made me come in.

Yes. That’s actually part of what scares me, because it wasn’t only when I had all that energy and wasn’t sleeping.

There was a time after that, when I kind of came down from it — like my energy dropped back down and I was sleeping more like my usual amount again — and I thought maybe the voices would stop because I wasn’t in that keyed-up state anymore. But they didn’t. They kept going. I’d be feeling more normal, not all revved up, just trying to go through my regular day, and I’d still hear them.

I’d be in my studio or around my things, and the voices would still come, and I still had that feeling that my work was being judged or tracked somehow. I remember thinking, “Why is this still happening if I’m sleeping again?” because I wanted to believe it was just from being overtired, but it kept happening even when my mood felt more normal.

mood.\n  manifestation: I go from feeling so bright and chosen that Greenville can’t hold all my plans to snapping hard at someone over a small thing, like a client asking me to repeat myself.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: I get so much energy that I’ll clean my whole kitchen, start three paintings, crochet half a blanket, and rewrite care schedules all in the same night without being able to sit down.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I truly believed my artwork was going to get discovered any day and that I was supposed to build a whole new care-and-art program that doctors and agencies would come learn from me.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: I’ve gone many nights sleeping maybe two or three hours, then getting up feeling charged like I had a full rest and going straight back to painting or making calls.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts run so fast that one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song, and I can’t slow it down enough to make sense.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I’ll try to finish a care note or a crochet row, but a car door outside, a bird at the window, or a color on the table pulls me away and I’m off doing something else before I know it.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was sleeping only a few hours and pushing my big art-and-care plans, I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling certain people were watching my work.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from that but still not back to myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I got to the point where my aide job was in danger because I missed shifts, mixed up care notes, and could not keep visits organized the way I used to. I also stopped going to my walking club and library art talks, so I was mostly alone at home.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, since my work hours were cut and my money situation got much worse.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The voices and fixed fears about my work being watched have been happening for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The stretches of being charged up, sleeping very little, and chasing big plans have been coming and going for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: During the time you’ve been hearing the voices, have you also had a period when you felt unusually high, energized, or euphoric for days at a time?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there was a stretch like that. I was pushing these big plans I had, like with my art and also care plans, like I was going to organize everything and make something really important out of it.\n\nAnd during that same time, the voices were happening in the studio too. I’d be in there working, barely sleeping, feeling like I had to keep going, and I’d hear them so clearly. And I also felt really certain that people were watching my work somehow, like it was being monitored or judged. It all kind of came together at the same time, the energy and the not sleeping and the voices.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard the voices during a time when your mood felt normal?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s actually part of what scares me, because it wasn’t only when I had all that energy and wasn’t sleeping.\n\nThere was a time after that, when I kind of came down from it — like my energy dropped back down and I was sleeping more like my usual amount again — and I thought maybe the voices would stop because I wasn’t in that keyed-up state anymore. But they didn’t. They kept going. I’d be feeling more normal, not all revved up, just trying to go through my regular day, and I’d still hear them.\n\nAnd it wasn’t just for a day or two. It went on for several weeks. I’d be in my studio or around my things, and the voices would still come, and I still had that feeling that my work was being judged or tracked somehow. I remember thinking, “Why is this still happening if I’m sleeping again?” because I wanted to believe it was just from being overtired, but it kept happening even when my mood felt more normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. Because I can get discouraged, definitely, but I haven’t really kept track of it in a neat way like that. I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a time when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. studio and feeling certain people were watching my work.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from That’s actually part of what scares me, because it wasn’t only when I had all that energy and wasn’t sleeping.\n\nThere was a time after that, when I kind of came down from it — like my energy dropped back down and I was sleeping more like my usual amount again — and I thought maybe the voices would stop because I wasn’t in that keyed-up state anymore. But they didn’t. They kept going. I’d be feeling more normal, not all revved up, just trying to go through my regular day, and I’d still hear them.\n\nAnd it wasn’t just for a day or two. I just know I’ve had periods where I felt really down and tired of everything, but I can’t pin it down the way you’re asking.\n\n[Last Question]\nBefore the voices started, had you been using drugs or substances that can affect the mind, like meth, cocaine, LSD, or a lot of cannabis?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I know what you’re asking, like whether something I took could have set it off, but I don’t want to say something wrong.

description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was sleeping only a few hours and pushing my big art-and-care plans, I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling certain people were watching my work.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from that but still not back to myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I got to the point where my aide job was in danger because I missed shifts, mixed up care notes, and could not keep visits organized the way I used to. I also stopped going to my walking club and library art talks, so I was mostly alone at home.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, since my work hours were cut and my money situation got much worse.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The voices and fixed fears about my work being watched have been happening for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The stretches of being charged up, sleeping very little, and chasing big plans have been coming and going for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had days where I’ve felt low or worn down by everything, like just tired of dealing with it, but I don’t know if it was most of the day nearly every day for two whole weeks.\n\nWhen you say depressed, do you mean like crying all the time, or not wanting to get out of bed, or just feeling heavy and discouraged? Because I can get discouraged, definitely, but I haven’t really kept track of it in a neat way like that. self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I truly believed my artwork was going to get discovered any day and that I was supposed to build a whole new care-and-art program that doctors and agencies would come learn from me.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: I’ve gone many nights sleeping maybe two or three hours, then getting up feeling charged like I had a full rest and going straight back to painting or making calls.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts run so fast that one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song, and I can’t slow it down enough to make sense.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I’ll try to finish a care note or a crochet row, but a car door outside, a bird at the window, or a color on the table pulls me away and I’m off doing something else before I know it.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was sleeping only a few hours and pushing my big art-and-care plans, I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling certain people were watching my work.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from that but still not back to myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I got to the point where my aide job was in danger because I missed shifts, mixed up care notes, and could not keep visits organized the way I used to. I also stopped going to my walking club and library art talks, so I was mostly alone at home.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, since my work hours were cut and my money situation got much worse.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The voices and fixed fears about my work being watched have been happening for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The stretches of being charged up, sleeping very little, and chasing big plans have been coming and going for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I still don’t know that I can say it that clearly, like “yes, for two weeks.” I’ve had low times, and I’ve had times where I felt worn out and kind of defeated, but I wasn’t marking it on a calendar, you know?\n\nSome days I’d feel heavy and discouraged for a big part of the day, and then maybe the next day I’d be more distracted or just trying to get through things. So I don’t want to tell you something wrong. When you say “most of the day nearly every day,” that sounds very specific, and I’m not sure I can honestly say I’ve had that for at least two weeks straight. I just know I’ve had periods where I felt really down and tired of everything, but I can’t pin it down the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, had you been using drugs or substances that can affect the mind, like meth, cocaine, LSD, or a lot of cannabis?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I know what you’re asking, like whether something I took could have set it off, but I don’t want to say something wrong.\n\nI wasn’t sitting there thinking, “Oh, this started right after some drug,” if that’s what you mean. I don’t really have a clear connection in my mind like that. And some of those things you listed, I’d have to be honest, I don’t really know all the categories or what counts as “a lot” with something like cannabis. I know people talk about substances like it’s very obvious, but for me I’m trying to think back to before the voices started and it’s kind of blurry in the sense of what mattered and what didn’t.\n\nSo… I can’t really point to meth or cocaine or LSD or heavy marijuana use and say, yes, that was happening before the voices. I just don’t have that to tell you. If there’s something specific you want me to go through, I can try, but I don’t remember it being tied to something like that.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor ever told you that you have a brain or neurological condition?\n\nPatient: Not that I know of. I mean, I don’t remember any doctor sitting me down and saying, “You have a brain condition” or “You have a neurological condition,” like something with the nerves or the brain itself.\n\nI’ve been to doctors for different things over the years, but if you mean like a diagnosis from a neurologist or something like that, I don’t think so. Sometimes medical words go by fast and I don’t always know what category they’re putting things in, so I don’t want to say absolutely never if it’s written somewhere and I didn’t understand it. But as far as what I’ve been told directly, no, I haven’t been told I have something like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nDid the voices start all of a sudden, within a couple of days?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I can say it was all of a sudden like that, like within just a couple of days. I don’t have it organized in my head that neatly.

When I think back on it, it’s hard to separate what started when, because at first I don’t know if I was even understanding what was happening. It wasn’t like I wrote down, “This is day one.” I just remember becoming aware of it, and then looking back and wondering if it had been creeping in before I really admitted it to myself.

So if you mean did I wake up one day and then two days later it was clearly there, I can’t honestly say that. I don’t know. It feels more blurry than sudden to me, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m sure about the timeline when I’m not.

Yes… yes, they did. It’s been way longer than a month.

I remember thinking, “Why is this still happening if I’m sleeping again?” because I wanted to believe it was just from being overtired, but it kept happening even when my mood felt more normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had days where I’ve felt low or worn down by everything, like just tired of dealing with it, but I don’t know if it was most of the day nearly every day for two whole weeks.\n\nWhen you say depressed, do you mean like crying all the time, or not wanting to get out of bed, or just feeling heavy and discouraged? Because I can get discouraged, definitely, but I haven’t really kept track of it in a neat way like that. I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I still don’t know that I can say it that clearly, like “yes, for two weeks.” I’ve had low times, and I’ve had times where I felt worn out and kind of defeated, but I wasn’t marking it on a calendar, you know?\n\nSome days I’d feel heavy and discouraged for a big part of the day, and then maybe the next day I’d be more distracted or just trying to get through things. So I don’t want to tell you something wrong. When you say “most of the day nearly every day,” that sounds very specific, and I’m not sure I can honestly say I’ve had that for at least two weeks straight. I just know I’ve had periods where I felt really down and tired of everything, but I can’t pin it down the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, had you been using drugs or substances that can affect the mind, like meth, cocaine, LSD, or a lot of cannabis?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I know what you’re asking, like whether something I took could have set it off, but I don’t want to say something wrong.\n\nI wasn’t sitting there thinking, “Oh, this started right after some drug,” if that’s what you mean. I don’t really have a clear connection in my mind like that. And some of those things you listed, I’d have to be honest, I don’t really know all the categories or what counts as “a lot” with something like cannabis. I know people talk about substances like it’s very obvious, but for me I’m trying to think back to before the voices started and it’s kind of blurry in the sense of what mattered and what didn’t.\n\nSo… I can’t really point to meth or cocaine or LSD or heavy marijuana use and say, yes, that was happening before the voices. I just don’t have that to tell you. If there’s something specific you want me to go through, I can try, but I don’t remember it being tied to something like that.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor ever told you that you have a brain or neurological condition?\n\nPatient: Not that I know of. I mean, I don’t remember any doctor sitting me down and saying, “You have a brain condition” or “You have a neurological condition,” like something with the nerves or the brain itself.\n\nI’ve been to doctors for different things over the years, but if you mean like a diagnosis from a neurologist or something like that, I don’t think so. Sometimes medical words go by fast and I don’t always know what category they’re putting things in, so I don’t want to say absolutely never if it’s written somewhere and I didn’t understand it. But as far as what I’ve been told directly, no, I haven’t been told I have something like that.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices start all of a sudden, within a couple of days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it was all of a sudden like that, like within just a couple of days. I don’t have it organized in my head that neatly.\n\nWhen I think back on it, it’s hard to separate what started when, because at first I don’t know if I was even understanding what was happening. It wasn’t like I wrote down, “This is day one.” I just remember becoming aware of it, and then looking back and wondering if it had been creeping in before I really admitted it to myself.\n\nSo if you mean did I wake up one day and then two days later it was clearly there, I can’t honestly say that. I don’t know. It feels more blurry than sudden to me, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m sure about the timeline when I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices continue for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, they did. It’s been way longer than a month.\n\nI mean, the voices and the fears I have about my work being watched, that’s been going on for about nine months now. It’s hard for me to say it like a clean start date, like I said before, but once it was happening, it didn’t just stop after a few days or a couple weeks. It kept going.\n\nAnd I know a month is what you’re asking, but in my head it feels like it’s taken up such a big stretch of time that I almost lose track of how long it’s been. I’m not really sure. I know there are times where things don’t feel enjoyable like they used to, but I don’t know if I can say it was almost everything, for at least two weeks, in that clear way.

Like, sometimes I’ll stop wanting to do things, or I just don’t feel much from them, but then I don’t know if that’s because I’m tired, or stressed, or just not in the mood. I don’t keep track day by day, so when you ask “two weeks,” I start trying to count it in my head and it gets kind of foggy.

But they didn’t. They kept going. I’d be feeling more normal, not all revved up, just trying to go through my regular day, and I’d still hear them.\n\nAnd it wasn’t just for a day or two. It went on for several weeks. I’d be in my studio or around my things, and the voices would still come, and I still had that feeling that my work was being judged or tracked somehow. I remember thinking, “Why is this still happening if I’m sleeping again?” because I wanted to believe it was just from being overtired, but it kept happening even when my mood felt more normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had days where I’ve felt low or worn down by everything, like just tired of dealing with it, but I don’t know if it was most of the day nearly every day for two whole weeks.\n\nWhen you say depressed, do you mean like crying all the time, or not wanting to get out of bed, or just feeling heavy and discouraged? Because I can get discouraged, definitely, but I haven’t really kept track of it in a neat way like that. I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I still don’t know that I can say it that clearly, like “yes, for two weeks.” I’ve had low times, and I’ve had times where I felt worn out and kind of defeated, but I wasn’t marking it on a calendar, you know?\n\nSome days I’d feel heavy and discouraged for a big part of the day, and then maybe the next day I’d be more distracted or just trying to get through things. So I don’t want to tell you something wrong. When you say “most of the day nearly every day,” that sounds very specific, and I’m not sure I can honestly say I’ve had that for at least two weeks straight. I just know I’ve had periods where I felt really down and tired of everything, but I can’t pin it down the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, had you been using drugs or substances that can affect the mind, like meth, cocaine, LSD, or a lot of cannabis?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I know what you’re asking, like whether something I took could have set it off, but I don’t want to say something wrong.\n\nI wasn’t sitting there thinking, “Oh, this started right after some drug,” if that’s what you mean. I don’t really have a clear connection in my mind like that. And some of those things you listed, I’d have to be honest, I don’t really know all the categories or what counts as “a lot” with something like cannabis. I know people talk about substances like it’s very obvious, but for me I’m trying to think back to before the voices started and it’s kind of blurry in the sense of what mattered and what didn’t.\n\nSo… I can’t really point to meth or cocaine or LSD or heavy marijuana use and say, yes, that was happening before the voices. I just don’t have that to tell you. If there’s something specific you want me to go through, I can try, but I don’t remember it being tied to something like that.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor ever told you that you have a brain or neurological condition?\n\nPatient: Not that I know of. I mean, I don’t remember any doctor sitting me down and saying, “You have a brain condition” or “You have a neurological condition,” like something with the nerves or the brain itself.\n\nI’ve been to doctors for different things over the years, but if you mean like a diagnosis from a neurologist or something like that, I don’t think so. Sometimes medical words go by fast and I don’t always know what category they’re putting things in, so I don’t want to say absolutely never if it’s written somewhere and I didn’t understand it. But as far as what I’ve been told directly, no, I haven’t been told I have something like that.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices start all of a sudden, within a couple of days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it was all of a sudden like that, like within just a couple of days. I don’t have it organized in my head that neatly.\n\nWhen I think back on it, it’s hard to separate what started when, because at first I don’t know if I was even understanding what was happening. It wasn’t like I wrote down, “This is day one.” I just remember becoming aware of it, and then looking back and wondering if it had been creeping in before I really admitted it to myself.\n\nSo if you mean did I wake up one day and then two days later it was clearly there, I can’t honestly say that. I don’t know. It feels more blurry than sudden to me, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m sure about the timeline when I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices continue for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, they did. It’s been way longer than a month.\n\nI mean, the voices and the fears I have about my work being watched, that’s been going on for about nine months now. It’s hard for me to say it like a clean start date, like I said before, but once it was happening, it didn’t just stop after a few days or a couple weeks. It kept going.\n\nAnd I know a month is what you’re asking, but in my head it feels like it’s taken up such a big stretch of time that I almost lose track of how long it’s been. But yes, definitely at least a month. More like around nine months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I’ve definitely had times where I felt really low, like heavy and worn down, and it can feel like it’s on me for a while. But if you’re asking specifically, like, two full weeks where it was most of the day, nearly every day… I’m not sure I’ve kept track of it that way.\n\nSometimes the days kind of run together, and I don’t always notice until later that I’ve been feeling bad for a stretch. But I don’t want to say yes if I can’t honestly remember it being that exact kind of pattern. I guess I’d say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you lost interest or pleasure in almost everything for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know there are times where things don’t feel enjoyable like they used to, but I don’t know if I can say it was almost everything, for at least two weeks, in that clear way.\n\nLike, sometimes I’ll stop wanting to do things, or I just don’t feel much from them, but then I don’t know if that’s because I’m tired, or stressed, or just not in the mood. I don’t keep track day by day, so when you ask “two weeks,” I start trying to count it in my head and it gets kind of foggy.\n\nSo I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I guess the honest answer is I don’t know if it’s been that specific.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{"candidates":["F25.0","F25.9","F20.0"],"note":"","is_final":false}

I know there are times where things don’t feel enjoyable like they used to, but I don’t know if I can say it was almost everything, for at least two weeks, in that clear way.\n\nLike, sometimes I’ll stop wanting to do things, or I just don’t feel much from them, but then I don’t know if that’s because I’m tired, or stressed, or just not in the mood. I don’t keep track day by day, so when you ask “two weeks,” I start trying to count it in my head and it gets kind of foggy.\n\nSo I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I guess the honest answer is I don’t know if it’s been that specific.\n\n[Last Question]\nDuring those low periods, did you ever feel sad or down most of the day nearly every day?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I can say it was most of the day nearly every day. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt down, yes, like emotionally tired and kind of weighed down, but when you say “most of the day” and “nearly every day,” I start second-guessing myself.

Some days I might wake up feeling bad and it stays with me, but other days it kind of comes and goes, or I get distracted by what I have to do. And then later I’ll think, “Was I sad all day, or was I just overwhelmed?” It’s hard for me to separate it out like that.

So I guess the honest answer is I’m not sure. I know I’ve had low periods, but I can’t clearly remember if it was sad or down most of the day nearly every day.

But they didn’t. They kept going. I’d be feeling more normal, not all revved up, just trying to go through my regular day, and I’d still hear them.\n\nAnd it wasn’t just for a day or two. I just don’t have that to tell you. I mean, I don’t remember any doctor sitting me down and saying, “You have a brain condition” or “You have a neurological condition,” like something with the nerves or the brain itself.\n\nI’ve been to doctors for different things over the years, but if you mean like a diagnosis from a neurologist or something like that, I don’t think so. painting or making calls.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts run so fast that one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song, and I can’t slow it down enough to make sense.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I’ll try to finish a care note or a crochet row, but a car door outside, a bird at the window, or a color on the table pulls me away and I’m off doing something else before I know it.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was sleeping only a few hours and pushing my big art-and-care plans, I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling certain people were watching my work.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from that but still not back to myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I got to the point where my aide job was in danger because I missed shifts, mixed up care notes, and could not keep visits organized the way I used to. I also stopped going to my walking club and library art talks, so I was mostly alone at home.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, since my work hours were cut and my money situation got much worse.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The voices and fixed fears about my work being watched have been happening for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The stretches of being charged up, sleeping very little, and chasing big plans have been coming and going for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I’ve definitely had times where I felt really low, like heavy and worn down, and it can feel like it’s on me for a while. But if you’re asking specifically, like, two full weeks where it was most of the day, nearly every day… I’m not sure I’ve kept track of it that way.\n\nSometimes the days kind of run together, and I don’t always notice until later that I’ve been feeling bad for a stretch. But I don’t want to say yes if I can’t honestly remember it being that exact kind of pattern. I guess I’d say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you lost interest or pleasure in almost everything for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t keep track day by day, so when you ask “two weeks,” I start trying to count it in my head and it gets kind of foggy.\n\nSo I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I guess the honest answer is I don’t know if it’s been that specific.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you ever feel sad or down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say it was most of the day nearly every day. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt down, yes, like emotionally tired and kind of weighed down, but when you say “most of the day” and “nearly every day,” I start second-guessing myself.\n\nSome days I might wake up feeling bad and it stays with me, but other days it kind of comes and goes, or I get distracted by what I have to do. And then later I’ll think, “Was I sad all day, or was I just overwhelmed?” It’s hard for me to separate it out like that.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I’m not sure. I know I’ve had low periods, but I can’t clearly remember if it was sad or down most of the day nearly every day.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt hopeless nearly every day?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t know if I can answer that as a clear yes. Hopeless is a strong word, and I’m trying to think if there was a stretch where I felt that way nearly every day for two weeks straight, but I don’t really keep track of my feelings in that kind of calendar way.

I guess what I can say is I’ve felt low at times, but I’m not sure about a two-week period of feeling hopeless nearly every day.

I just don’t have that to tell you. If there’s something specific you want me to go through, I can try, but I don’t remember it being tied to something like that.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor ever told you that you have a brain or neurological condition?\n\nPatient: Not that I know of. I mean, I don’t remember any doctor sitting me down and saying, “You have a brain condition” or “You have a neurological condition,” like something with the nerves or the brain itself.\n\nI’ve been to doctors for different things over the years, but if you mean like a diagnosis from a neurologist or something like that, I don’t think so. Sometimes medical words go by fast and I don’t always know what category they’re putting things in, so I don’t want to say absolutely never if it’s written somewhere and I didn’t understand it. But as far as what I’ve been told directly, no, I haven’t been told I have something like that.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices start all of a sudden, within a couple of days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it was all of a sudden like that, like within just a couple of days. It kept going.\n\nAnd I know a month is what you’re asking, but in my head it feels like it’s taken up such a big stretch of time that I almost lose track of how long it’s been. If there’s something specific you want me to go through, I can try, but I don’t remember it being tied to something like that.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor ever told you that you have a brain or neurological condition?\n\nPatient: Not that I know of. I mean, I don’t remember any doctor sitting me down and saying, “You have a brain condition” or “You have a neurological condition,” like something with the nerves or the brain itself.\n\nI’ve been to doctors for different things over the years, but if you mean like a diagnosis from a neurologist or something like that, I don’t think so. Sometimes medical words go by fast and I don’t always know what category they’re putting things in, so I don’t want to say absolutely never if it’s written somewhere and I didn’t understand it. But as far as what I’ve been told directly, no, I haven’t been told I have something like that.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices start all of a sudden, within a couple of days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it was all of a sudden like that, like within just a couple of days. I don’t have it organized in my head that neatly.\n\nWhen I think back on it, it’s hard to separate what started when, because at first I don’t know if I was even understanding what was happening. It wasn’t like I wrote down, “This is day one.” I just remember becoming aware of it, and then looking back and wondering if it had been creeping in before I really admitted it to myself.\n\nSo if you mean did I wake up one day and then two days later it was clearly there, I can’t honestly say that. I don’t know. It feels more blurry than sudden to me, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m sure about the timeline when I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices continue for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, they did. It’s been way longer than a month.\n\nI mean, the voices and the fears I have about my work being watched, that’s been going on for about nine months now. It’s hard for me to say it like a clean start date, like I said before, but once it was happening, it didn’t just stop after a few days or a couple weeks. It kept going.\n\nAnd I know a month is what you’re asking, but in my head it feels like it’s taken up such a big stretch of time that I almost lose track of how long it’s been. But yes, definitely at least a month. More like around nine months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I’ve definitely had times where I felt really low, like heavy and worn down, and it can feel like it’s on me for a while. But if you’re asking specifically, like, two full weeks where it was most of the day, nearly every day… I’m not sure I’ve kept track of it that way.\n\nSometimes the days kind of run together, and I don’t always notice until later that I’ve been feeling bad for a stretch. But I don’t want to say yes if I can’t honestly remember it being that exact kind of pattern. I guess I’d say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you lost interest or pleasure in almost everything for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know there are times where things don’t feel enjoyable like they used to, but I don’t know if I can say it was almost everything, for at least two weeks, in that clear way.\n\nLike, sometimes I’ll stop wanting to do things, or I just don’t feel much from them, but then I don’t know if that’s because I’m tired, or stressed, or just not in the mood. I don’t keep track day by day, so when you ask “two weeks,” I start trying to count it in my head and it gets kind of foggy.\n\nSo I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I guess the honest answer is I don’t know if it’s been that specific.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you ever feel sad or down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say it was most of the day nearly every day. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt down, yes, like emotionally tired and kind of weighed down, but when you say “most of the day” and “nearly every day,” I start second-guessing myself.\n\nSome days I might wake up feeling bad and it stays with me, but other days it kind of comes and goes, or I get distracted by what I have to do. I don’t know. It feels more blurry than sudden to me, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m sure about the timeline when I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices continue for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, they did. It’s been way longer than a month.\n\nI mean, the voices and the fears I have about my work being watched, that’s been going on for about nine months now. It’s hard for me to say it like a clean start date, like I said before, but once it was happening, it didn’t just stop after a few days or a couple weeks. It kept going.\n\nAnd I know a month is what you’re asking, but in my head it feels like it’s taken up such a big stretch of time that I almost lose track of how long it’s been. But yes, definitely at least a month. More like around nine months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I’ve definitely had times where I felt really low, like heavy and worn down, and it can feel like it’s on me for a while. But if you’re asking specifically, like, two full weeks where it was most of the day, nearly every day… I’m not sure I’ve kept track of it that way.\n\nSometimes the days kind of run together, and I don’t always notice until later that I’ve been feeling bad for a stretch. But I don’t want to say yes if I can’t honestly remember it being that exact kind of pattern. I guess I’d say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you lost interest or pleasure in almost everything for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know there are times where things don’t feel enjoyable like they used to, but I don’t know if I can say it was almost everything, for at least two weeks, in that clear way.\n\nLike, sometimes I’ll stop wanting to do things, or I just don’t feel much from them, but then I don’t know if that’s because I’m tired, or stressed, or just not in the mood. I don’t keep track day by day, so when you ask “two weeks,” I start trying to count it in my head and it gets kind of foggy.\n\nSo I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I guess the honest answer is I don’t know if it’s been that specific.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you ever feel sad or down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say it was most of the day nearly every day. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt down, yes, like emotionally tired and kind of weighed down, but when you say “most of the day” and “nearly every day,” I start second-guessing myself.\n\nSome days I might wake up feeling bad and it stays with me, but other days it kind of comes and goes, or I get distracted by what I have to do. Hopeless is a strong word, and I’m trying to think if there was a stretch where I felt that way nearly every day for two weeks straight, but I don’t really keep track of my feelings in that kind of calendar way.\n\nI’ve had times where things felt really hard, like I couldn’t see my way through them, but then there might be moments where I’m just numb, or distracted, or getting through work or whatever I have to do. So I don’t know if that counts as hopeless nearly every day, or if it was more like feeling worn down and stuck.\n\nI guess what I can say is I’ve felt low at times, but I’m not sure about a two-week period of feeling hopeless nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gone through a time when you were crying much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I’ve cried at times, sure, but “much more than usual” is hard for me to judge, because I’m not someone who keeps track of that. Sometimes I’ll tear up when I’m alone or when I’m just overwhelmed, but then other times I kind of hold it in, especially around people, because I don’t like feeling watched or having to explain myself.\n\nSo if you’re asking was there a clear period where I was crying all the time or noticeably more than normal, I’m not sure I can say that. I don’t remember it in a clear way like, “yes, this week or those weeks I cried every day.” It’s more blurry than that. I know I’ve had emotional moments, but I don’t know if it was much more than usual.\n\n[Last Question]\nWas there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though you were not having a high-energy or depressed mood episode?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Additional Requirement 3\nmanifestation: \"There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, I think so. There was a time like that, where the high-energy part had kind of settled down already. Like I wasn’t running around with all that extra energy anymore, and my sleep had gone more back to normal — I was sleeping more regular again, not like before.

But even after that, the voices didn’t just stop. They kept going for a while, like several weeks. And it wasn’t like I was in one of those really low times either, at least not that I can clearly say. I wasn’t feeling especially up or especially depressed the whole time, but I was still hearing them.

And along with that, I still had this feeling — or belief, I guess — that people were judging me or watching how I was doing my work, like tracking what I was doing. It felt like they were paying attention to me in a way that wasn’t normal. That kept going even after my mood and sleep seemed more normal. So yes, there was a stretch where those things were still happening on their own.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF25.0, F25.9, F20.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, because I keep hearing voices, and it’s gotten so clear that I can’t just brush it off anymore. It sounds like somebody is standing right there in my little studio with me, like not a thought in my head, not like remembering somebody’s voice — I mean clear, out loud, like a person in the room.\n\nThey’ll say my name, and then they’ll say things about my work being judged. And I’ll stop what I’m doing and look around, because it feels that real, but the house is quiet. Nobody’s there. I check, and there’s no one. It’s really upsetting because I’ll be trying to go about my day, and then there it is again, like I’m being watched or evaluated somehow. That’s what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: During the time you’ve been hearing the voices, have you also had a period when you felt unusually high, energized, or euphoric for days at a time?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there was a stretch like that. I don’t know if I’d call it “high” exactly, but I was running on almost no sleep, just a few hours, and I felt like I had all this energy and this big purpose. I was pushing these big plans I had, like with my art and also care plans, like I was going to organize everything and make something really important out of it.\n\nAnd during that same time, the voices were happening in the studio too. I’d be in there working, barely sleeping, feeling like I had to keep going, and I’d hear them so clearly. And I also felt really certain that people were watching my work somehow, like it was being monitored or judged. It all kind of came together at the same time, the energy and the not sleeping and the voices.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard the voices during a time when your mood felt normal?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s actually part of what scares me, because it wasn’t only when I had all that energy and wasn’t sleeping.\n\nThere was a time after that, when I kind of came down from it — like my energy dropped back down and I was sleeping more like my usual amount again — and I thought maybe the voices would stop because I wasn’t in that keyed-up state anymore. But they didn’t. They kept going. I’d be feeling more normal, not all revved up, just trying to go through my regular day, and I’d still hear them.\n\nAnd it wasn’t just for a day or two. It went on for several weeks. I’d be in my studio or around my things, and the voices would still come, and I still had that feeling that my work was being judged or tracked somehow. I remember thinking, “Why is this still happening if I’m sleeping again?” because I wanted to believe it was just from being overtired, but it kept happening even when my mood felt more normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had days where I’ve felt low or worn down by everything, like just tired of dealing with it, but I don’t know if it was most of the day nearly every day for two whole weeks.\n\nWhen you say depressed, do you mean like crying all the time, or not wanting to get out of bed, or just feeling heavy and discouraged? Because I can get discouraged, definitely, but I haven’t really kept track of it in a neat way like that. I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I still don’t know that I can say it that clearly, like “yes, for two weeks.” I’ve had low times, and I’ve had times where I felt worn out and kind of defeated, but I wasn’t marking it on a calendar, you know?\n\nSome days I’d feel heavy and discouraged for a big part of the day, and then maybe the next day I’d be more distracted or just trying to get through things. So I don’t want to tell you something wrong. When you say “most of the day nearly every day,” that sounds very specific, and I’m not sure I can honestly say I’ve had that for at least two weeks straight. I just know I’ve had periods where I felt really down and tired of everything, but I can’t pin it down the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, had you been using drugs or substances that can affect the mind, like meth, cocaine, LSD, or a lot of cannabis?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, I know what you’re asking, like whether something I took could have set it off, but I don’t want to say something wrong.\n\nI wasn’t sitting there thinking, “Oh, this started right after some drug,” if that’s what you mean. I don’t really have a clear connection in my mind like that. And some of those things you listed, I’d have to be honest, I don’t really know all the categories or what counts as “a lot” with something like cannabis. I know people talk about substances like it’s very obvious, but for me I’m trying to think back to before the voices started and it’s kind of blurry in the sense of what mattered and what didn’t.\n\nSo… I can’t really point to meth or cocaine or LSD or heavy marijuana use and say, yes, that was happening before the voices. I just don’t have that to tell you. If there’s something specific you want me to go through, I can try, but I don’t remember it being tied to something like that.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor ever told you that you have a brain or neurological condition?\n\nPatient: Not that I know of. I mean, I don’t remember any doctor sitting me down and saying, “You have a brain condition” or “You have a neurological condition,” like something with the nerves or the brain itself.\n\nI’ve been to doctors for different things over the years, but if you mean like a diagnosis from a neurologist or something like that, I don’t think so. Sometimes medical words go by fast and I don’t always know what category they’re putting things in, so I don’t want to say absolutely never if it’s written somewhere and I didn’t understand it. But as far as what I’ve been told directly, no, I haven’t been told I have something like that.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices start all of a sudden, within a couple of days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it was all of a sudden like that, like within just a couple of days. I don’t have it organized in my head that neatly.\n\nWhen I think back on it, it’s hard to separate what started when, because at first I don’t know if I was even understanding what was happening. It wasn’t like I wrote down, “This is day one.” I just remember becoming aware of it, and then looking back and wondering if it had been creeping in before I really admitted it to myself.\n\nSo if you mean did I wake up one day and then two days later it was clearly there, I can’t honestly say that. I don’t know. It feels more blurry than sudden to me, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m sure about the timeline when I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices continue for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, they did. It’s been way longer than a month.\n\nI mean, the voices and the fears I have about my work being watched, that’s been going on for about nine months now. It’s hard for me to say it like a clean start date, like I said before, but once it was happening, it didn’t just stop after a few days or a couple weeks. It kept going.\n\nAnd I know a month is what you’re asking, but in my head it feels like it’s taken up such a big stretch of time that I almost lose track of how long it’s been. But yes, definitely at least a month. More like around nine months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I’ve definitely had times where I felt really low, like heavy and worn down, and it can feel like it’s on me for a while. But if you’re asking specifically, like, two full weeks where it was most of the day, nearly every day… I’m not sure I’ve kept track of it that way.\n\nSometimes the days kind of run together, and I don’t always notice until later that I’ve been feeling bad for a stretch. But I don’t want to say yes if I can’t honestly remember it being that exact kind of pattern. I guess I’d say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you lost interest or pleasure in almost everything for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know there are times where things don’t feel enjoyable like they used to, but I don’t know if I can say it was almost everything, for at least two weeks, in that clear way.\n\nLike, sometimes I’ll stop wanting to do things, or I just don’t feel much from them, but then I don’t know if that’s because I’m tired, or stressed, or just not in the mood. I don’t keep track day by day, so when you ask “two weeks,” I start trying to count it in my head and it gets kind of foggy.\n\nSo I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I guess the honest answer is I don’t know if it’s been that specific.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you ever feel sad or down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say it was most of the day nearly every day. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt down, yes, like emotionally tired and kind of weighed down, but when you say “most of the day” and “nearly every day,” I start second-guessing myself.\n\nSome days I might wake up feeling bad and it stays with me, but other days it kind of comes and goes, or I get distracted by what I have to do. And then later I’ll think, “Was I sad all day, or was I just overwhelmed?” It’s hard for me to separate it out like that.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I’m not sure. I know I’ve had low periods, but I can’t clearly remember if it was sad or down most of the day nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt hopeless nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can answer that as a clear yes. Hopeless is a strong word, and I’m trying to think if there was a stretch where I felt that way nearly every day for two weeks straight, but I don’t really keep track of my feelings in that kind of calendar way.\n\nI’ve had times where things felt really hard, like I couldn’t see my way through them, but then there might be moments where I’m just numb, or distracted, or getting through work or whatever I have to do. So I don’t know if that counts as hopeless nearly every day, or if it was more like feeling worn down and stuck.\n\nI guess what I can say is I’ve felt low at times, but I’m not sure about a two-week period of feeling hopeless nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gone through a time when you were crying much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I don’t remember it in a clear way like, “yes, this week or those weeks I cried every day.” It’s more blurry than that. So yes, there was a stretch where those things were still happening on their own.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m not really sure how to answer that one clearly. I’ve had times where I felt down, like really heavy and worn out inside, but when you say “for two weeks in a row” and “nearly every day,” I get stuck because I don’t remember it in that exact kind of way.

I know there are times where things don’t feel enjoyable like they used to, but I don’t know if I can say it was almost everything, for at least two weeks, in that clear way.\n\nLike, sometimes I’ll stop wanting to do things, or I just don’t feel much from them, but then I don’t know if that’s because I’m tired, or stressed, or just not in the mood. I don’t keep track day by day, so when you ask “two weeks,” I start trying to count it in my head and it gets kind of foggy.\n\nSo I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I guess the honest answer is I don’t know if it’s been that specific.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you ever feel sad or down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say it was most of the day nearly every day. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt down, yes, like emotionally tired and kind of weighed down, but when you say “most of the day” and “nearly every day,” I start second-guessing myself.\n\nSome days I might wake up feeling bad and it stays with me, but other days it kind of comes and goes, or I get distracted by what I have to do. And then later I’ll think, “Was I sad all day, or was I just overwhelmed?” It’s hard for me to separate it out like that.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I’m not sure. I know I’ve had low periods, but I can’t clearly remember if it was sad or down most of the day nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt hopeless nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can answer that as a clear yes. Hopeless is a strong word, and I’m trying to think if there was a stretch where I felt that way nearly every day for two weeks straight, but I don’t really keep track of my feelings in that kind of calendar way.\n\nI’ve had times where things felt really hard, like I couldn’t see my way through them, but then there might be moments where I’m just numb, or distracted, or getting through work or whatever I have to do. So I don’t know if that counts as hopeless nearly every day, or if it was more like feeling worn down and stuck.\n\nI guess what I can say is I’ve felt low at times, but I’m not sure about a two-week period of feeling hopeless nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gone through a time when you were crying much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I’ve cried at times, sure, but “much more than usual” is hard for me to judge, because I’m not someone who keeps track of that. Sometimes I’ll tear up when I’m alone or when I’m just overwhelmed, but then other times I kind of hold it in, especially around people, because I don’t like feeling watched or having to explain myself.\n\nSo if you’re asking was there a clear period where I was crying all the time or noticeably more than normal, I’m not sure I can say that. I don’t remember it in a clear way like, “yes, this week or those weeks I cried every day.” It’s more blurry than that. I know I’ve had emotional moments, but I don’t know if it was much more than usual.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though you were not having a high-energy or depressed mood episode?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. There was a time like that, where the high-energy part had kind of settled down already. Like I wasn’t running around with all that extra energy anymore, and my sleep had gone more back to normal — I was sleeping more regular again, not like before.\n\nBut even after that, the voices didn’t just stop. They kept going for a while, like several weeks. And it wasn’t like I was in one of those really low times either, at least not that I can clearly say. I wasn’t feeling especially up or especially depressed the whole time, but I was still hearing them.\n\nAnd along with that, I still had this feeling — or belief, I guess — that people were judging me or watching how I was doing my work, like tracking what I was doing. It felt like they were paying attention to me in a way that wasn’t normal. That kept going even after my mood and sleep seemed more normal. So yes, there was a stretch where those things were still happening on their own.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when, for two weeks in a row, you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one clearly. I’ve had times where I felt down, like really heavy and worn out inside, but when you say “for two weeks in a row” and “nearly every day,” I get stuck because I don’t remember it in that exact kind of way.\n\nThere have been stretches where I wasn’t myself, where getting through the day felt harder and I didn’t feel good emotionally, but I don’t know if I would call it depressed every single day, or almost every day, for a full two weeks. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I can say that exactly. I mean, I’ve had times where I didn’t feel like doing much, where things just felt kind of flat or like too much effort, but “nearly all activities” for two weeks straight… I’m not sure.

Sometimes medical words go by fast and I don’t always know what category they’re putting things in, so I don’t want to say absolutely never if it’s written somewhere and I didn’t understand it. But as far as what I’ve been told directly, no, I haven’t been told I have something like that.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices start all of a sudden, within a couple of days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it was all of a sudden like that, like within just a couple of days. I don’t have it organized in my head that neatly.\n\nWhen I think back on it, it’s hard to separate what started when, because at first I don’t know if I was even understanding what was happening. It wasn’t like I wrote down, “This is day one.” I just remember becoming aware of it, and then looking back and wondering if it had been creeping in before I really admitted it to myself.\n\nSo if you mean did I wake up one day and then two days later it was clearly there, I can’t honestly say that. I don’t know. It feels more blurry than sudden to me, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m sure about the timeline when I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices continue for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, they did. It’s been way longer than a month.\n\nI mean, the voices and the fears I have about my work being watched, that’s been going on for about nine months now. It’s hard for me to say it like a clean start date, like I said before, but once it was happening, it didn’t just stop after a few days or a couple weeks. It kept going.\n\nAnd I know a month is what you’re asking, but in my head it feels like it’s taken up such a big stretch of time that I almost lose track of how long it’s been. But yes, definitely at least a month. More like around nine months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. But if you’re asking specifically, like, two full weeks where it was most of the day, nearly every day… I’m not sure I’ve kept track of it that way.\n\nSometimes the days kind of run together, and I don’t always notice until later that I’ve been feeling bad for a stretch. I don’t keep track day by day, so when you ask “two weeks,” I start trying to count it in my head and it gets kind of foggy.\n\nSo I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I guess the honest answer is I don’t know if it’s been that specific.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you ever feel sad or down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say it was most of the day nearly every day. Yes… I think that’s probably closer to what happens with me. Like, when I’m not all wound up or going nonstop, I can just kind of sit there and nothing feels worth doing.

I’ll have my coffee in the morning, and I mean I’ll just sit there so long it gets cold, and I don’t even care enough to warm it back up. My phone will ring or people will call, and I’ll look at it and just not answer. Not even because I’m busy, just because I don’t feel like talking or getting into anything.

And things I usually do like… like the walking club, I’ll just stop going. I know it would probably be good for me to get out, and normally I like seeing people there, but in those times I just don’t care enough to make myself go. Same with painting. I can have my paintbrush right there, and I won’t even pick it up. It’s not even like I’m thinking, “Oh, I hate this now.” It’s more like there’s no pull toward it, no enjoyment waiting for me in it.

So yes, I guess there have been times like that, where things I usually enjoy just don’t feel like anything to me.
